B routine priorityPublic-health news

Health District reports first Clade I mpox case in Clark County resident - Yahoo

News Brief
The Southern Nevada Health District confirmed the first case of clade I mpox in a Clark County resident on October 18, 2026. This marks a recent increase in mpox cases, with 11 confirmed clade II cases reported since August 2026. The Health District has documented 365 mpox cases since the first case in June 2022. The outbreak in Central and Eastern Africa since 2024 has also been noted, with cases identified in other countries, including the United States. The district emphasizes the importance of vaccination, especially for those over 18, and advises individuals who may have been exposed to seek guidance promptly. Prevention measures include avoiding close contact, wearing masks, and isolating until all sores have healed. The release highlights that mpox spreads through close physical contact, including sexual contact, and can be prevented with the JYNNEOS vaccine. The Health District encourages people at risk to get vaccinated and those exposed to monitor for symptoms for 21 days.
Original source text (English)
LAS VEGAS (KLAS) -- The Southern Nevada Health District announced Tuesday the first confirmed case of clade I mpox in a Clark County resident. The district said the person has no reported travel exposure. >>Download the free 8 News+ app for live newscasts and streaming originals Additionally, the Health District reported 11 confirmed cases of clade II mpox since August 2026. This marks a recent increase in cases. "This first clade I case and the recent increase in clade II cases are reminders that mpox continues to circulate in our community," Dr. Cassius Lockett, District Health Officer for the Health District, said. "We encourage people at risk to get vaccinated and anyone who may have been exposed to seek guidance promptly. Recognizing symptoms and avoiding close contact when sick can also help prevent further spread." Mpox is caused by two types of the virus, which are known as clade I and clade II. Clade II is the type that caused the global outbreak that started in 2022. That outbreak continues to circulate at low levels in the United States, the health district release said. A large clade I outbreak has spread widely in Central and Eastern Africa since 2024, with cases also identified in other countries, such as the United States. Both types of the virus can be prevented in the same way, and the JYNNEOS is expected to protect against both. The Health District offers mpox vaccines for those over 18 who are eligible at immunization clinics. The vaccine is given in two doses, four weeks apart. Appointments are available at this link . Those who may have been exposed should contact a health care provider about a vaccine and monitor for any symptoms for 21 days after the last exposure. "Mpox spreads primarily through close, sustained physical contact, including sexual or intimate contact," the release said. "It can spread through direct contact with a person's rash, sores, scabs or bodily fluids, including during kissing or oral, anal or vaginal sex. It can also spread through contact with contaminated items, such as clothing, bedding or towels, and during pregnancy or childbirth." Symptoms can begin within three weeks of exposure and illness may last two to four weeks. A rash may appear as bumps or blisters on or near the genitals or anus, or on other areas such as the hands, feet, chest, face or mouth. Other symptoms can include fever, chills, swollen lymph nodes, headache, muscle aches, and fatigue. Some people develop a rash without other symptoms. People with suspected or confirmed mpox should take the following steps to prevent spreading the illness: Stay home and avoid close contact with others, including people and pets in the same household. Avoid sexual contact, kissing, and other close physical contact. If being around others is unavoidable, wear a well-fitting mask and completely cover rashes and sores with clothing or bandages. Avoid sharing clothing, bedding, towels and other personal items. Wash hands frequently with soap and water, especially after touching the rash. Use an alcohol-based hand sanitizer if soap and water are unavailable. People with mpox should continue isolation until all sores have healed, scabs have fallen off, and a fresh layer of skin has formed. Since the first mpox case was reported in Clark County in June 2022, the Health District has reported 365 cases. Copyright 2026 Nexstar Media, Inc. All rights reserved. 南非的 猴痘:病毒如何传播以及如何保护自己 - KAYA 959

Mpox in South Africa: How the virus spreads and how to protect yourself - KAYA 959
新闻简报南非又确认了 4 例 猴痘 病例,使五个省份的病例总数达到 26 例。卫生部通过实验室检测报告了这些病例,其中西开普省有 18 例,豪登省 3 例,东开普省 2 例,自由邦省 2 例,夸祖鲁-纳塔尔省 1 例。在这 26 例病例中,25 例为男性,年龄在 23 至 50 岁之间,中位年龄为 37 岁。有 17 例病例的分支和谱系数据,其中 13 例与分支 Ib 相关,4 例与分支 IIb 相关。建议公众避免与疑似病例密切接触,监测接触后的症状,如有疑虑,请就医。卫生部正在与国家传染病研究所 (NICD) 和省级当局合作,加强 监测、接触者追踪和社区参与。敦促医护人员保持高度怀疑并遵守感染控制协议。暴发 强调需要保持警惕并及时进行医疗干预,以防止进一步传播。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 10 月 25 日,卫生部确认南非新增 4 例 猴痘 病例。据报道,五个省份的病例总数为 26 例。
地点与对象
南非五个省份记录了病例:西开普省(18例)、豪登省(3例)、东开普省(2例)、自由邦省(2例)和夸祖鲁-纳塔尔省(1例)。26例中,25例为男性,年龄在23至50岁之间,中位年龄37岁。
事件
南非新增 4 例 猴痘 病例,使五个省份的病例总数达到 26 例。卫生部通过实验室检测证实了这些病例。
规模、影响与风险
南非实验室确诊的 猴痘 病例总数为 26 例。有 17 例病例的分支和谱系信息,其中 13 例与分支 Ib 相关,4 例与分支 IIb 相关。敦促公众监测接触后的症状,如有疑虑,请寻求医疗建议。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生部已确认这些病例,并正在与国家传染病研究所 (NICD)、省卫生当局和其他利益相关者合作,加强 监测、病例发现、接触者追踪、风险沟通和社区参与。建议医护人员保持高度怀疑并遵守感染预防和控制方案。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mpox in South Africa: How the virus spreads and how to protect yourself - KAYA 959

News Brief
South Africa has confirmed four additional mpox cases, raising the total to 26 across five provinces. The Department of Health reported these cases through laboratory testing, with 18 in the Western Cape, three in Gauteng, two in the Eastern Cape, two in the Free State, and one in KwaZulu-Natal. Of the 26 cases, 25 are male, aged between 23 to 50 years, with a median age of 37. Clade and lineage data is available for 17 cases, with 13 linked to Clade Ib and four to Clade IIb. The public is advised to avoid close contact with suspected cases, monitor symptoms after exposure, and seek medical attention if concerned. The Department of Health is collaborating with the National Institute for Communicable Diseases (NICD) and provincial authorities to enhance surveillance, contact tracing, and community engagement. Healthcare workers are urged to maintain a high index of suspicion and follow infection control protocols. The outbreak highlights the need for vigilance and timely medical intervention to prevent further spread.

Listen live app Music streaming subscription Drive time show Listen to Radio Morning talk show Mpox in South Africa: How the virus spreads and how to protect yourself By Mapaballo Borotho Image @WHO South Africa has recorded four additional mpox cases, bringing the total to 26 across five provinces. Symptoms include a rash, fever, headache, muscle aches, swollen lymph nodes and fatigue. The public is urged to avoid close contact with suspected cases, monitor symptoms after exposure and seek medical advice if concerned. The Department of Health has confirmed four additional cases of mpox in South Africa, bringing the total number of laboratory-confirmed cases in the country to 26. Of the 26 cases recorded, 18 were reported in the Western Cape, three in Gauteng, two in the Eastern Cape, two in the Free State and one in KwaZulu-Natal. Health Department spokesperson Foster Mohale said two of the 18 cases in the Western Cape involved foreign nationals who were visiting South Africa, while the remaining 16 were linked to local transmission. “Twenty-five of the 26 cases are male, aged between 23 to 50 years and a median age of 37 years. Clade and lineage information is currently available for 17 cases, of which 13 are associated with the Clade Ib mpox virus and four with Clade IIb,” Mohale said. Members of the public are urged to remain vigilant and seek medical attention if they develop symptoms associated with the illness. What are the symptoms of mpox? The Department of Health has advised the public to look out for the following symptoms: A rash, which may develop into blisters or sores. Fever. Headache. Muscle aches. Swollen lymph nodes. Fatigue. Anyone who suspects they have been in close contact with someone who has mpox is urged to seek medical advice and monitor themselves for symptoms for 21 days. “Should symptoms develop, they should avoid close contact with others and seek medical attention,” Mohale added. How can you protect yourself against mpox? Individuals should avoid close, skin-to-skin contact with anyone who has an mpox-like rash. People should also avoid sharing bedding, towels, clothing and other personal items with someone who has the infection. Healthcare workers are urged to maintain a high index of suspicion when assessing suspected cases and to follow infection prevention and control protocols. “The Department will continue to work closely with the National Institute for Communicable Diseases (NICD), provincial health authorities and other stakeholders to strengthen surveillance, case finding, contact tracing, risk communication and community engagement in order to contain further spread of mpox,” Mohale said. What is mpox? According to the World Health Organisation (WHO), mpox, previously known as monkeypox, is a viral illness caused by the monkeypox virus, which belongs to the Orthopoxvirus genus. The virus has two distinct clades: clade I, which includes subclades Ia and Ib, and clade II, which includes subclades IIa and IIb. The 2022–2023 global mpox outbreak was caused by the clade IIb strain. More recently, an increase in cases in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and other countries linked to clades Ia and Ib has raised international concern. How is mpox transmitted? Mpox can spread through close physical contact with someone who has the infection, including skin-to-skin contact and contact with lesions, bodily fluids or contaminated materials such as clothing and bedding. Transmission can also occur through respiratory particles during prolonged close contact. In healthcare settings, exposure through needlestick injuries is another possible route. The virus can also be transmitted during pregnancy or birth. Infection during pregnancy can pose serious risks to the fetus or newborn, including pregnancy loss, stillbirth and other complications. People who have multiple sexual partners may have a higher risk of exposure, particularly where close physical contact occurs with someone who has the infection. Who is at risk of mpox? According to the WHO, groups that may face a higher risk of exposure include: Healthcare and care workers who may come into contact with the virus. People living in the same household or close community as someone with mpox, including children. People with multiple sexual partners, including men who have sex with men. Sex workers of any gender and their clients. However, mpox can affect anyone who has been exposed to the virus. It is important not to stigmatise people based on their gender, sexuality, occupation or nationality. The WHO says most people with mpox recover within two to four weeks after seeking medical attention. Anyone who suspects they have the infection should seek medical advice promptly, follow healthcare guidance and avoid close contact with others while awaiting assessment. Location and population
The cases were recorded in five provinces of South Africa: Western Cape (18 cases), Gauteng (3 cases), Eastern Cape (2 cases), Free State (2 cases), and KwaZulu-Natal (1 case). Of the 26 cases, 25 are male, aged between 23 to 50 years, with a median age of 37 years.
Event
South Africa has recorded four additional mpox cases, bringing the total to 26 across five provinces. The Department of Health confirmed these cases through laboratory testing.
Scale, impact and risk
The total number of laboratory-confirmed mpox cases in South Africa is 26. Clade and lineage information is available for 17 cases, with 13 associated with Clade Ib and four with Clade IIb. The public is urged to monitor symptoms after exposure and seek medical advice if concerned.
Response, status and uncertainty

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Tally hits 95 as two more monkeypox cases reported in Sindh - The Express Tribune

News Brief
In Sindh, Pakistan, two additional monkeypox cases were reported on October 22, 2026, raising the total caseload to 95. The virus has caused 11 deaths this year. The patients, Salman Ali and Ishrat Fatima, tested positive at the Sindh Infectious Diseases Hospital and are currently receiving treatment. Of the 95 cases, 71 were men and 24 were women, with 23 children under nine also affected. The latest cases highlight the need for continued surveillance and preventive measures to curb the spread of the virus. Officials and experts have emphasized the importance of limiting close contact with infected individuals and seeking immediate medical attention if symptoms such as fever, swollen lymph nodes, or unusual skin rashes appear. The situation remains under active monitoring with a focus on containment and public health guidance.

巴基斯坦发现四例 猴痘 病例,没有出国旅行 — Dawn - UA.NEWS

Four mpox cases detected in Pakistan without foreign travel — Dawn - UA.NEWS
新闻简报据UA.NEWS报道,巴基斯坦开伯尔-普赫图赫瓦省新增4例猴痘病例,无一人有出国旅行史。这些病例是通过开伯尔医科大学公共卫生参考实验室的检测发现的,周六结果呈阳性。受影响的人包括一对已婚夫妇,其中一名女性和三名男性目前处于家庭隔离状态。卫生工作者对当地 传播 的可能性表示担忧,因为情况被描述为令人震惊。卫生部门已启动接触者追踪,并计划对患者的近亲进行检测。今年以来,全省共报告猴痘病例18例,死亡1例,治愈13例。医生指出,死者可能有潜在的健康问题,专家敦促采取加强感染控制和防止重复使用注射器等措施来遏制病毒传播。
查看新闻五要素
时间
这四例 猴痘 病例于 2026 年 10 月确诊,周六检测结果呈阳性,该省首例病例于 2023 年记录。
地点与对象
这些病例是在开伯尔-普赫图赫瓦省发现的,特别是在瑙谢拉、白沙瓦和马尔丹。受影响的人群包括四人,其中一名女性和三名男性正在家庭隔离。
事件
巴基斯坦开伯尔-普赫图赫瓦省新增4例猴痘病例,无一人有出国旅行史。卫生工作者担心病毒可能在当地传播。
规模、影响与风险
今年以来,该省猴痘病例已增至18例。其中1名女性和3名男性处于家庭隔离状态,患者中已有1人死亡,另有13人康复。医生认为死者可能患有潜在的健康问题。卫生工作者表示,由于社区内出现 传播 的可能性,情况令人担忧。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生部门计划追踪患者的接触者并对他们的近亲进行检测。医疗机构已收到关于从疑似患有 猴痘 的人身上收集样本进行实验室确认的建议。专家呼吁加强感染控制,防止重复使用注射器,并在发现新患者的地区扩大筛查范围。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Four mpox cases detected in Pakistan without foreign travel — Dawn - UA.NEWS

News Brief
Four new cases of mpox have been confirmed in Pakistan’s Khyber Pakhtunkhwa province, with none of the patients having a history of foreign travel, according to a report by UA.NEWS. The cases were identified through testing at the Public Health Reference Laboratory of Khyber Medical University, with results coming back positive on Saturday. The affected individuals include a married couple, with one woman and three men currently in home isolation. Health workers have expressed concern over the potential for local transmission, as the situation is described as alarming. The health department has initiated contact tracing and plans to test close relatives of the patients. Since the beginning of the year, the province has reported 18 mpox cases, with one death and 13 recoveries. Doctors noted that the deceased may have had underlying health conditions, and specialists have urged measures such as strengthening infection control and preventing the reuse of syringes to curb the spread of the virus.
Original source text (English)
News Health Four mpox cases detected in Pakistan without foreign travel — Dawn UA.NEWS 25 October 2026 07:41 Four new cases of mpox have been confirmed in Pakistan’s Khyber Pakhtunkhwa province, including in a married couple. None of the patients had a history of foreign travel, Dawn reports . Health workers fear possible local spread of the virus. Samples from the patients, which were sent by Khyber Teaching Hospital last week, were examined by the Public Health Reference Laboratory of Khyber Medical University. The mpox test results came back positive on Saturday. One woman and three men are in home isolation. Contact tracing Two patients live in Nowshera, while one each lives in Peshawar and Mardan. The health department plans to trace the patients’ contacts and test their close relatives. Healthcare facilities, especially in the areas where the cases were recorded, have already received recommendations on collecting samples from people suspected of having mpox for laboratory confirmation. More current news is available on the UA.News Telegram channel Telegram . According to the health department’s surveillance system, following the latest confirmations, the number of mpox cases in the province since the beginning of the year has risen to 18. There has been one death among the patients, while 13 people have recovered. At the same time, doctors suggested that the deceased person may have had underlying health conditions. Health workers described the situation as alarming due to the likelihood of transmission within communities. Prevention measures Previously, mpox cases in Khyber Pakhtunkhwa were mostly linked to international travel: infected people contracted the disease abroad before entering Pakistan. The first case in the province was recorded in 2023 in a driver who worked in Saudi Arabia and tested positive after arriving. Specialists called for strengthening infection control, preventing the reuse of syringes, and expanding screening in the areas where new patients were identified. According to Mehran Khan, head of the dermatology department at Khyber Teaching Hospital, mpox can be transmitted through direct contact with a patient’s blood, body fluids, skin lesions or mucous membranes, as well as through contaminated objects, including bedding. Time
The four mpox cases were confirmed in October 2026, with test results coming back positive on Saturday, and the first case in the province was recorded in 2023.
Location and population
The cases were detected in Khyber Pakhtunkhwa province, specifically in Nowshera, Peshawar, and Mardan. The affected population includes four individuals, with one woman and three men in home isolation.
Event
Four new cases of mpox were confirmed in Pakistan’s Khyber Pakhtunkhwa province, with none of the patients having a history of foreign travel. Health workers fear possible local spread of the virus.
Scale, impact and risk
Since the beginning of the year, the number of mpox cases in the province has risen to 18. One woman and three men are in home isolation, and there has been one death among the patients, while 13 people have recovered. Doctors suggested that the deceased person may have had underlying health conditions. Health workers described the situation as alarming due to the likelihood of transmission within communities.
Response, status and uncertainty
The health department plans to trace the patients’ contacts and test their close relatives. Healthcare facilities have received recommendations on collecting samples from people suspected of having mpox for laboratory confirmation. Specialists called for strengthening infection control, preventing the reuse of syringes, and expanding screening in the areas where new patients were identified.
